     1	# LinkedIn post — draft
     2	
     3	Plain text below the rule; LinkedIn renders no Markdown. `<BLOG-URL>` is a
     4	placeholder until the blog post has its address.
     5	
     6	---
     7	
     8	Is confidential transfer possible on the XRP Ledger? Yes, on Devnet only: read on 2026-10-22, the ConfidentialTransfer amendment (XLS-96) was enabled on Devnet, and not on Mainnet or Testnet.
     9	
    10	It hides less than the word suggests. I built the core of a receivables facility on it and ran it on Devnet to find out exactly what an outside observer sees.
    11	
    12	Hidden from the public: each holder's balance, and the amount of each transfer between confidential balances.
    13	
    14	Public: who pays whom, when, and how often. Every amount converted into or out of confidential form. Some zero balances, which anyone can recognise.
    15	
    16	The issuer can read every balance, always. No setting turns that off.
    17	
    18	There is no key rotation or recovery in XLS-96, or on Mainnet, Testnet or Devnet today. A draft standard, XLS-99, proposes both.
    19	
    20	In the facility, that means the size of the float, which suppliers drew on which days, and every retirement tranche are public. Each supplier's limit, the size of each drawdown and what each supplier owes are not. It hides the shape of the book, not its size.
    21	
    22	Along the way, eleven issues went upstream to XRPLF repositories. Four were resolved in September, and a fifth was fixed on 2026-10-21, not yet in a release.
    23	
    24	This is a research project on a test network. It is not audited, and not production-ready.
    25	
    26	The write-up: <BLOG-URL>
    27	A read-only demo: https://xrpl-confidential-positions.vercel.app
    28	The code and the claims register: https://github.com/nandanito/xrpl-confidential-positions
    29	
    30	XLS-96 is the work of Murat Cenk, Aanchal Malhotra, Ayo Akinyele, Peter Chen, Shawn Xie and Yinyi Qian, with Tess Dore and Murat Cenk's compact sigma protocol.
22:| P-1 | That this work makes XRPL quantum-safe or quantum-resistant | It does not |
23:| P-2 | That an application-layer PQ scheme protects funds at the ledger level | An adversary who breaks secp256k1 signs a valid classical transaction and the network accepts it |
24:| P-3 | That any amendment is live or enabled, without a dated `feature` RPC check | Amendment state is a reading, not knowledge |
25:| P-4 | That a Confidential MPT demo hides identities | It hides amounts and balances. Sender and receiver remain public |
26:| P-5 | That anything here is trustless, non-custodial, decentralised, audited, production-ready or secure | None of it is; ADR-0001 names the trusted parties |
27:| P-6 | That anything is compliant with regulation in a named jurisdiction | Not a compliance project |
28:| P-7 | That analysis of published work is original research | Cite Cenk, Malhotra, Akinyele, Chen, Xie, Qian by name where the work is theirs. **Add Tess Dore (2026-08-16):** the compact sigma protocol, the twisted-ElGamal analysis and the Bulletproofs++ analysis are Dore & Cenk, RippleX Research — Dore is *not* an XLS-0096 author and is easy to omit by copying E-1's list |
29:| P-8 | Any benchmark number without hardware, parameter set and methodology | Includes re-quoting XLS-0096 §14.2's "laptop-class CPU" timings — see B-1 |
30:| P-9 | That holders are private from the issuer | The issuer's decryption mirror is mandatory. No configuration makes this false |
31:| P-10 | That XLS-96 supports key rotation or key recovery | It supports neither, at any level |
32:| P-11 | That confidential transfers hide *who* transacts, or *when*, or *how often* | Only amounts are hidden. The counterparty graph and event timing are fully public |
33:| P-12 | **Any claim that a source does NOT say something — *nothing documents it*, *no such field*, *not exported*, *nobody has called it out*, *I could not find it* — without the search that would falsify it recorded alongside** | It is an assertion about a whole corpus and one search settles it. **Three graded rows failed this way on 2026-08-26** (E-155, E-156), two of them **positives**: U-15 quoted a sentence XLS-0096 has never contained; U-12 said a property was *"not called out anywhere I found"* when the spec states it **twice**, once in wording identical to the benefit claimed as an original reading. ⚠ **This shape survives review because it does not look like a claim** — a quotation has quotation marks, a benchmark visibly wants a methodology, and *"nobody has documented this"* reads as modesty. Gated by `tools/check-negative-claims.ts` |
108:| E-58 | **The auditor view is executed, not inferred.** With an issuance carrying `sfAuditorEncryptionKey`, the auditor's ElGamal key decrypts a holder's `sfAuditorEncryptedBalance` to the exact plaintext balance, matching what the holder reads with their own key and what the issuer reads from the issuer mirror. Read 500 immediately after the Convert, then **re-derived independently as 475 after a 25-unit Send** — the three views track each other through a balance change, which is the stronger statement | Executed 2026-08-17 on issuance `00453A7DD67EB7E60002C33AAD59E7F544C136A8D30F5D52`. **Three parties decrypt the same balance: holder, issuer, auditor — verified twice, at two different balances.** Reinforces P-9 and P-4 — this is a confidentiality model with mandatory issuer visibility and optional third-party visibility, and neither is hideable from the holder's side | A |
109:| E-59 | **The two harnesses CLAUDE.md requires now exist and both self-verify.** `tools/observer-harness.ts` holds **no keys and links nothing from mpt-crypto** — no vendored C library, no WASM module. It recomputes the canonical `EncZero` from three public inputs using general-purpose primitives only, `@noble/hashes` and `@noble/curves`. *(Corrected 2026-08-17: an earlier wording said "links no cryptography library", which was false — it links two.)*, and `--assert-encrypted-zero` **exits non-zero unless F-1 is demonstrated on live ledger state**. `tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts` is the single command CLAUDE.md specifies, taking its key from the environment or a file and never from argv | Executed 2026-08-17. **The reimplementation is cross-checked, not asserted**: it reproduces an on-chain `sfConfidentialBalanceSpending` byte-for-byte, and a perturbed-AccountID control matches nothing on the same object — so the match is evidence rather than coincidence. Negative controls pass: wrong key → exit 1, absent key → exit 2, unknown flag → exit 2, missing key file → exit 2 with no path in the message. ⚠ **The rejection-sampling retry branch is NOT covered by any test** — it fires with probability ~2⁻¹²⁸, so the live cross-check does not exercise it, and this row should not be read as validating it. The issuer tool read the same balance, 475, under **three different keys** (issuer, auditor, holder) from three different mirrors. **This is the first artifact in the project that would fail if a confidentiality claim were false**, which is what CLAUDE.md's testing rule asks for. **Extended 2026-08-18 with a second assertion, `--assert-no-r-reuse`** (E-63): it scans an account's confidential transactions for a `C₁` appearing in more than one transaction — reuse of the ElGamal randomness `r` — and, where found, recovers the difference between the two hidden amounts with no key. **Discriminates in both directions, executed**: exit 1 on the account whose transactions deliberately reused `r`, with the difference recovered from all three participant ciphertexts; exit 0 on two accounts that did not. It correctly does **not** flag `C₁` repeating *within* one Send, which is by design (E-4/F-13). This is a self-check as much as an audit — our own signer could regress into it and the ledger would not complain | A |
197:| E-146 | **Architecture §6 step 7 is BUILT AND EXECUTED — `facility/`, the receivables facility as application logic.** ⚠ **Executed under E-151's amendment, so `underB15Conditions: false`** — the ledger source was remote and this run says nothing about the loopback obligation. **Reviewed in two rounds, three legs → E-150.** ~~and it is NOT EXECUTED~~ — **struck 2026-08-25.** The Dedicated Account, the single conversion, supplier enrolment, drawdowns, repayments, the balance book and retirement in tranches. What the build actually enforces, as distinct from what it describes: **`LoopbackLedger` is the only ledger read path** and refuses a non-loopback literal *before it connects* — ⚠ **unless the risk has been accepted by name** (E-151's hatch, which is how the executed run happened, and which this clause omitted until round two) — so a misconfigured facility cannot tell a remote host the shape of its reads on the way to failing; **`FailHardSubmitter` is the only submission path for a facility movement** and has no method that can omit `fail_hard`, with the method set pinned by a test so a new path cannot appear unnoticed — ⚠ **and `run-facility.ts` §1's fail-hard control makes exactly one submission outside it, without the flag, on every run.** That is the point of a control and it is also why `underB15Conditions` is `false` on every run this driver makes; **`FacilityBook`** is TASK ZERO §9's R-5 balance-tracking requirement, and reports search **widths** rather than seconds because P-8 forbids a benchmark number without hardware, parameter set and methodology. ⚠ **The loopback check is STRICTER than the signer's**: a numeric literal in `127.0.0.0/8` or `::1` only, because a name is resolved by something the process does not control — `REVIEW-016`'s two bypasses and round two's unterminated `[::1` are negative test inputs rather than prose. ~~⚠ **NOT EXECUTED means no facility transaction has been submitted to any ledger**, and every capability sentence about it is therefore about code and tests, not about rippled's answer~~ — **struck 2026-08-26.** ⚠ **This sentence sat at the end of the row whose own heading says BUILT AND EXECUTED**, and survived `REVIEW-023` C-6's sweep of nine denials because the sweep worked from a list rather than from a re-read. Round two's Gemini leg graded it HIGH and was right to: a register row that contradicts itself is worse than a wrong one, because both readings are citable. **28 validated facility transactions, 2026-08-25** — see **B-18** | **2026-08-25.** **81 tests** (`bun test facility tools`, 2026-08-26) ⚠ **not the 63 this row claimed until round two** — 63 was already stale at `REVIEW-023`'s own tree, which runs **77**, a real type-check (`bunx tsc --noEmit` — added because `bun build` is not one, E-145), `tools/check-loopback.ts` PASS, and `tools/check-blinding-sources.ts` extended to walk `facility/` and still reporting 5 call sites, **with its coverage of the new tree spot-checked by planting a call and watching it fail**. ⚠ **A demo of the happy path is not evidence of confidentiality**, and the unit tests drive fake nodes — what a fake node proves is that the code does what the test says. What the Devnet run adds is ledger acceptance and the decrypted-balance agreement, and what it does **not** add is anything about the loopback obligation | **A, executed** for ledger acceptance and the decrypted positions; **no grade at all** for the loopback half of B-15, which this run did not test |
271:| E-211 | ⚠⚠ **A FALSE DISCLOSURE SENTENCE SHIPPED ON THE OBSERVER PAGE, AND IT IS F-24's SHAPE A THIRD TIME.** For any ciphertext not matching the canonical `EncZero`, the page said *"**Non-zero**, and the amount is hidden from an observer."* **That is false.** Measured on Devnet 2026-08-28: holder `rDYMkRMh7o7QfkNaoGvtd7TgFzmt12FEM8` decrypts to **0** through the issuer mirror, while its `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` does **not** match the canonical `EncZero` recomputed from its own registered key. ⚠⚠ **A BALANCE CAN BE EXACTLY ZERO AND NOT PUBLICLY IDENTIFIABLE AS ZERO.** F-1's canonical form is used on **first-time init, merge reset and clawback**; a balance that reached zero **by spending** is a homomorphic result — a randomised encryption of zero that matches nothing and looks identical to any other hidden value. ⚠⚠ **`tools/observer-harness.ts:277` HAD IT RIGHT AND THE WEB TIER FLATTENED THE HEDGE**: *"not the canonical zero (some non-zero value, or never reset)"* became *"Non-zero"*. **The correct source was one import away, in a file this module already imports `canonicalEncZero` from.** ⚠ **The wording tests did not catch it because it is a SEMANTIC error, not a phrasing one** — every guard was about privacy claims, and this is a claim about arithmetic. ⚠⚠ **AND THE PROTOCOL FACT WAS ALREADY IN THIS PROJECT'S OWN UPSTREAM REGISTER**: `UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md` **U-47** — *"a confidential balance that has been emptied by `ConvertBack` is not the canonical `EncZero`"* — observed on Devnet and written up before this tier existed. **So this is not a fact the project had to discover; it is one it had recorded, filed under its own heading, and then contradicted on a screen.** That is E-141's shape at the level of a whole register rather than a count | **Corrected 2026-08-28.** The note now states that a non-match means the value cannot be determined, that this is **not** the same as non-zero, and that a spent-to-zero balance looks exactly the same; F-1's line in *what is public* carries its condition; and the *what is hidden* entry no longer describes the hidden value as non-zero. ⚠ **The notes and both lists are now exported CONSTANTS and the tests assert on VALUES, not source text** — the first version scanned the file and failed on the **comment documenting this very bug**, which is E-182's shape inside a test. **Verified by breaking it**: reverting to the exact shipped sentence fails the suite, as does dropping F-1's condition | A, measured |
350:| E-290 | **The custody decision request priced the wrong set of credentials, and its first review round of its own withdrew its recommendation.** Three legs with differed packets found both halves independently, the specification-only leg included. First, the key that decrypts is also required to spend: every Send and ConvertBack proof takes the holder's ElGamal secret, so a confidential movement needs a signature and that secret together, and Option 2's *"movement moves to the quorum"* was false. Second, the Dedicated Account's key is not the only power over the float. The issuer can erase it with a confidential clawback and freeze it by enabling `CanLock` and locking, and the issuer's secret and the facility's plaintext book both read every ~~amount~~ confidential amount (E-295). Round two found a second freeze mechanism, requiring authorisation, also E-295. The option set also missed a regular key with the master disabled, which splits movement without a quorum, and delegation, a fourth authorisation path that nothing in this repository reads. ⚠ The recommendation, Option 1 with Option 2 costed and not built, is withdrawn and not replaced, because a replacement written by the reviewing session would be one more unreviewed input. | `docs/reviews/REVIEW-046c-adjudication-custody-request-2026-09-13.md` H1 to H4, each re-verified by the adjudicator after the legs returned. The proof witness is `mpt_utility.h:233-291` and `signer/src/prover.rs:724-733`, and XLS-0096 §5.5 at `58ccd30` says the holder key *"carries exclusive spending authority"*. At rippled `3.3.0` (`00a178f`): `ConfidentialMPTClawback.cpp:72-179`; `MPTokenIssuanceSet.cpp:142-197` for `CanLock` under `DynamicMPT`, read and not executed; `Transactor.cpp:1027-1042` for the regular key; `transactions.macro:1106-1136` and `Transactor.cpp:381-408` for delegation. `facility/run-facility.ts:323` sets `tfMPTCanClawback`, and `facility/src/book.ts:47-49` describes the book. `DynamicMPT` and `PermissionDelegationV1_1` were ENABLED on Devnet at 2026-09-13T13:37:26Z. Round two on the fixes is owed | A, source; the lock path read, not executed |
376:| E-316 | **Four of the 2026-08-26 filings were resolved upstream in September, in the merged XLS-0096 and the docs page, and the register did not know until a survey on 2026-10-18.** #3879 was closed on 2026-09-09 by dev-portal PR #3916; #608, #609 and #610 on 2026-09-10 by XLS-0096 PRs #604 and #628, the second being `58ccd3047`, the commit E-304 read four days later without noticing. 475:| F-17 | **`cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` Remark 6.1's stated justification is false, and a reader who trusts it builds a key-extraction bug.** The paper justifies its nonce construction with *"Including the full witness ensures nonces are unique per transaction."* **The witness is byte-identical across a resequenced or version-bumped resubmission of the same transfer**, while `TransactionContextID` — and therefore the challenge — is not. Deriving nonces over the paper's `statement` omits it, producing two proofs with shared commitments under distinct challenges. ⚠ **That reading of `statement` is not settled**: §6.2 enumerates the challenge hash's inputs with `TransactionContextID` as item 4 and closes *"Omitting any statement element from the hash would allow cross-protocol attacks"*, and upstream's prover names its variable `stmt_hash` and folds the context into it. The filing reports the ambiguity and its consequence, not the x reading alone (`REVIEW-048c` §A). The recommendation is sound; the reason given for it is not | **Found independently by two cryptography reviewers, 2026-08-17**, from the same primary sources with no sight of each other (REVIEW-005a §3.1, REVIEW-005b §0.1; adjudicated REVIEW-005c §1). ⚠ **Upstream's implementation is NOT affected on the utility path and is not relying on its salt**: `src/proof_compact_standard.c` hashes `context_id` into `stmt_hash` after the point block, as do the convertback, clawback and pok_sk provers — verified at `85dbac7`. **But the guard is `if (context_id)` and `proof_pok_sk.c` documents it as `/* context_id is optional */`, so a caller passing NULL reintroduces the full bug.** ⚠ Corrected 2026-10-18: it does not. `generate_deterministic_nonces` has mixed a fresh `RAND_bytes` salt into every call since #18 in April 2026, so a NULL context ID omits the context from the hash but does not repeat nonces (E-315). File as a paper correction plus a caller-error surface, not as an implementation defect. Ruled to file as an erratum (E-308). **Filed 2026-10-18 as [mpt-crypto#153](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/153)** after three review rounds, as the ambiguity and its consequence, with the caller-error half dropped (E-315) | A |
476:| F-18 | **The published Rust FFI crate silently links an mpt-crypto four tag-generations behind rippled's, and it is not on the mainline branch.** `mpt-crypto-sys@0.1.0-beta.1` sets `MPT_CRYPTO_VERSION = "0.3.0-rc2"` and downloads a prebuilt-dylib release tarball under a SHA-256 pin. `0.3.0-rc2` (commit `83d9b13`) is **older than the `0.4.0-rc4` shipped in rippled 3.3.0**, and older than `1.0.2` and `1.0.4`. **Two distinct failure modes, and they are opposite in character.** (i) Left alone, `cargo build` **succeeds** and links `0.3.0-rc2` — the crate's vendored `secp256k1_mpt.h` is **byte-identical** to the header in the tarball it fetches, so nothing is inconsistent and nothing warns; you simply get an old prover. (ii) Redirected at a current library via `MPT_CRYPTO_LIB_DIR` (→ E-61), the drift becomes loud: the `0.3.0-rc2` header declares **seven functions that no longer exist at `1.0.4`** — `secp256k1_elgamal_pedersen_link_prove`/`_verify`, `secp256k1_equality_plaintext_prove`/`_verify`, `secp256k1_mpt_proof_equality_shared_r_size`, `secp256k1_mpt_prove_equality_shared_r`, `secp256k1_mpt_verify_equality_shared_r` — so a caller touching any of them gets undefined symbols. **Upstream removed exported functions between the pinned version and current**, which is the direction that matters: the stale side has *more* API, not less. Separately, both crates were published from `XRPLF/xrpl-rust` commit `d36ef38`, path `internal/mpt-crypto-sys`, and **`d36ef38` is the head of the unmerged branch `mpt-crypto-beta-release` only** — the path does not exist on `main` or at the `v1.2.0` release | Verified 2026-08-18 by downloading the crate and the `0.3.0-rc2` natives tarball (20 MB, prebuilt dylibs for six platforms; the fetch path **works**, contrary to a first reading here that it might 404), diffing headers, and resolving the publish commit's branch membership via the GitHub API. 482:| F-23 | **A wrong ledger read is a CONFIDENTIALITY break, not a rejected transaction — and the signer cannot control which.** A `ConfidentialMPTSend` encrypts the amount to the destination's `HolderEncryptionKey` and the issuer's `IssuerEncryptionKey`, both read from the ledger. rippled verifies against the keys **it** holds — `ConfidentialMPTSend.cpp:132` takes `(*sleDestinationMPToken)[sfHolderEncryptionKey]` — so a substituted key fails the proof. It fails with **`tecBAD_PROOF`** (`ConfidentialTransfer.cpp:451`; `TER.h:371` makes it 199, a `tec`), and `Transactor.cpp:1653-1656` with `isTecClaimHardFail` (`applySteps.h:40`) **applies a `tec` to the ledger** unless the submitter set `tapFailHard`. So an adversary who controls what the signer reads gets the holder to publish the amount encrypted under a key the adversary holds, **and the failed transaction keeps it on chain**. The signer does not submit, so it does not get to set that flag | ~~**Read at source 2026-08-19.** ⚠ **Not executed**~~ — **EXECUTED 2026-08-19 and it HELD.** `tools/spike-step2-2026-08-19/f23-substituted-key.ts` built a Send encrypting the amount to an adversary's key instead of the destination's registered one. `tecBAD_PROOF`, **recorded on validated ledger 4603815**, hash `7A283EE5920EBB97AB90F4BC92B4E4E293C6DCE541A9607277D45DA4145AF160`. The amount **42 was recovered from public ledger data by the adversary**, holding only the key they supplied; **the intended destination could not decrypt it at all**. The published ciphertext is byte-identical to what was submitted. ⚠ **This is what a wrong READ costs. It is not a network attack** — nothing was compromised. Mitigated, not closed: TLS is required for any non-loopback endpoint, the signer re-reads the destination key before signing, and it checks the ledger's `HolderEncryptionKey` for **its own** account against the key it holds — **there is no such anchor for the destination's key or the issuer's** (B-15) | A, executed |
485:| F-19 | **mpt-crypto's exported PoK API makes account binding optional, and the public header does not say so.** `secp256k1_mpt_pok_sk_verify(ctx, proof, pk, context_id)` accepts a NULL `context_id` — `/* context_id is optional */` in `proof_pok_sk.c`, and `build_pok_challenge` folds the context in only `if (context_id)`. With NULL, the challenge is `SHA-256("CMPT_POK_SK_REGISTER" ‖ pk ‖ T)`: a valid registration proof bound to **nothing but the public key**, and therefore **replayable by any party who has seen it, on any account, on any chain**. The **public header** `include/secp256k1_mpt.h:219–224` declares both `_prove` and `_verify` with the parameter and ~~**no doc comment whatsoever**~~ no doc comment that mentions `context_id` (E-296) — the optionality and its cost are visible only by reading the `.c`. ⚠ **This does NOT affect the ledger.** rippled cannot reach the NULL path: the utility wrapper `mpt_verify_convert_proof` rejects a NULL context hash before forwarding (E-66), and rippled always supplies a `uint256`. **Never describe this as an XLS-96 vulnerability.** The exposure is to *other* consumers of the exported library — a Rust or WASM binding, or this project's own signer harness — for whom passing NULL compiles, runs, verifies, and silently produces a forgeable registration | **Read at source 2026-08-18** at `85dbac7` = `1.0.4`: `proof_pok_sk.c` (`build_pok_challenge`, both `MPT_ARG_CHECK` blocks), `include/secp256k1_mpt.h:211–224`, `src/utility/mpt_utility.cpp:864`. Checked 2026-09-14: the report raises this in Appendices B and D, its fix review does not track either, and upstream has documented the field as optional on one function but not made it required (E-296). 488:| F-12 | **mpt-crypto's shipped WASM build script fails at emsdk 6.0.6 and succeeds at its CI-pinned 6.0.2.** `.github/scripts/build-wasm.sh` links the final module with `emcc`, but `src/utility/mpt_utility.cpp` is C++, so the link needs libc++. At **6.0.6 it fails** with `undefined symbol: operator new(unsigned long)`, `__cxa_throw`, `vtable for std::length_error` and four more. Emscripten's own warning names the fix — *"Try linking with 'em++' or passing '-sDEFAULT_TO_CXX'"* — and adding `-sDEFAULT_TO_CXX` to the two `emcc` link lines is sufficient at `85dbac7` (at `1.0.5` there are three, `build-wasm.sh:363`, `:365` and `:374`, the third linking the browser glue; E-299): nothing else in the script needed changing. ⚠ **Narrowed 2026-08-17, self-caught before review. The first wording said "fails on any emsdk newer than its CI pin"** — an extrapolation from a single newer version. **Two versions were tested, not a range**; where between 6.0.2 and 6.0.6 the behaviour changes is unknown, and so is anything above 6.0.6. ⚠ Both are now known: 6.0.3, 6.0.4 and 6.0.5 build, and 6.0.6, 6.0.10 and 6.0.12 fail, because Emscripten 6.0.6 disabled `DEFAULT_TO_CXX` by default, and all three link lines need `em++` (E-315) | Executed 2026-08-17 at `85dbac7`, macOS/arm64. Both versions built from the same source tree; 6.0.2 produced `mpt_crypto.wasm` (2 110 544 B), 6.0.6 failed at step 5 and produced nothing. **The pin is doing real work and is not documented as load-bearing** — the comment beside `version: 6.0.2` says only "pinned for reproducible wasm output … the emsdk version does not change crypto results", which is true and understates it. Anyone following the script's own header ("Prerequisites: Emscripten SDK on PATH") without reading the CI workflow gets a link failure. Still holds at `1.0.5` on reading, not re-run (E-299). Re-run 2026-09-28 and 2026-10-18 at `3baaaac`, with the second run's logs kept under `docs/evidence/f12-emsdk-2026-10-18/`. Ruled to file (E-302). **Filed 2026-10-18 as [mpt-crypto#154](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/154)** after two review rounds, the first reproducing the builds independently (E-315) | A |
489:| F-13 | **`cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` §2.1's "transmitted once on-chain" is not true of rippled 3.3.0.** The paper's Table 2 totals a Send at 1177 B for n=4 on the basis that all ciphertexts share `C₁` and it is sent once. `C₁` **is** shared, and sharing is **protocol-enforced, not a property of our client**: a Send carrying independent per-participant randomness is refused by the prover, by `mpt_verify_send_proof`, and by rippled with `tecBAD_PROOF`, against a shared-`C₁` control returning `tesSUCCESS` (REVIEW-004c §3). But each field is a full 66-byte pair on the wire, so `C₁` is transmitted **once per participant**. Real size is **1210 B without an auditor and 1276 B with one, both measured**; the 99 B gap is exactly the three redundant `C₁` copies at n=4 | Executed 2026-08-17 → E-4, E-45. Two readings are open and this session cannot separate them: either the paper describes an intended optimisation the implementation has not taken, or it describes the protocol loosely and the wire format was never meant to match. **Settle which before filing** — as a size-optimisation suggestion it is worth ~99 B/tx; as an erratum it is a one-line correction. Check whether an `sfSharedC1`-style layout was considered. ⚠ Not settled, and the filing asks upstream which: correct the totals, or mark the layout a proposal the protocol did not adopt (E-315). Still holds at both upstream HEADs on reading, and the sizes were not re-measured (E-304). Ruled to file as an erratum (E-308). **Filed 2026-10-18 as [mpt-crypto#155](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/155)**, led by XLS-0096 §17.1, which computes 1276 and 1210 bytes outright (E-315) | A |
490:| F-15 | **The published JS wrapper ~~makes the tight-window decryption optimisation unreachable~~ keeps the tight-window decryption optimisation out of its hex API, reachable only through raw WASM calls (E-301).** The C API is `mpt_decrypt_amount(ct, priv, out, range_low, range_high)`; `@xrplf/mpt-crypto@0.1.0` exposes `decryptAmount(ciphertext, privateKey, rangeHigh)` and **fixes `range_low` to 0 internally**. So JS cost is always O(rangeHigh) from zero, and F-7's central result — that a 10¹⁶ balance decrypts in ~5 ms given a narrow window, because cost tracks window *width* not value magnitude — ~~cannot be obtained from the published API at all~~ cannot be obtained from `decryptAmount`, and can be from the exported `loadWasmModule` (E-301). An integrator tracking expected balances off-ledger (which F-7/U-21 say they must) has nowhere to put that knowledge | Executed 2026-08-17 against `@xrplf/mpt-crypto@0.1.0`, decrypting a live Devnet ciphertext whose true value is 150. Indicative timings, Apple M3 Pro, **n=1 per point, not a benchmark under P-8**: `rangeHigh` 1 000 → 42.6 ms; 100 000 → 485.6 ms; 1 000 000 → 4 259.6 ms — linear, as the fixed floor implies. The same ciphertext with a native `[149,151]` window returns in 21.6 ms *including process spawn*. **The structural claim, not the timings, is the finding**: `range_low` is absent from the wrapper's signature. Verified by reading `dist/esm/primitives.js:66–79` and `dist/primitives.d.ts:33`, so it is not an inference from timing. `index.d.ts`, which exports `loadWasmModule`, was not cited, and a run through it on 2026-09-14 reached the narrow window (E-301). Ruled not-to-file (E-302) | A on the API shape; timings indicative only |
491:| F-14 | **`tecINSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` misattributes the cause when a holder's balance is partly confidential — on vault AND lending transactors.** A holder owning 150 confidential units of an issuance, with 50 public units left, was refused a 100-unit `VaultDeposit` with *"Not enough funds available to complete requested transaction."* The holder **does** own the units; they are in a form the vault cannot see. The error names the wrong thing, and an integrator has no signal pointing at confidentiality | **Reproduced on a second, independent transactor 2026-08-17**: a borrower holding 5 public and 600 confidential units was refused a 120-unit `LoanPay` with the identical code and message. So this is not a quirk of one vault handler — it is what the whole stack does when value is held confidentially. Executed → E-50, F-10. **This is the concrete, integrator-visible face of F-10** and it is stronger evidence than F-10's current form, because it is a wrong answer rather than a missing warning. Same filing constraint applies: settle F-10's competing reading first (an issuance may legitimately be confidential-capable while a vault operates on its public balance), since if that reading holds this is an error-message request rather than a composability defect | A, executed (E-162) |
508:| R-1 | TASK ZERO §9 rev 1: the restructured facility "shows nothing about which supplier drew how much" | Amounts are hidden; counterparty graph, event timing and drawdown frequency are public | Found independently by all three review passes. Sender and receiver are public on every transfer |
509:| R-2 | ADR-0001 rev 1: local proving is "the only option under which the confidentiality claim is true" | Local proving preserves spending authority; issuer visibility is unchanged | Conflated custody with confidentiality — the exact distinction the ADR exists to draw |
510:| R-3 | F-3: "docs say confidentiality can be toggled on and off" | Withdrawn 2026-08-16 | Corrected upstream. The canonical xrpl.org page states enabling is one-way |
511:| R-4 | F-4: "docs say clawback resets the version counter" | Withdrawn 2026-08-16 | Not a doc error. TOB-RIPCTX-11, a Medium implementation bug found by Trail of Bits in April 2026 and since Resolved. The prerelease docs described pre-fix behaviour |
512:| R-6 | B-11 / ADR-0002 blocker 3 / investigation §12: that encrypted loan accounting is **structurally** blocked, because XLS-96's additive homomorphism "cannot support amortisation, which multiplies a balance by a rate" | Withdrawn 2026-08-17. The residual true statement is B-11 as rewritten | **The mathematics was wrong.** Scalar multiplication by a *public* scalar is native to an additively homomorphic scheme: for `(C₁,C₂) = (rG, mG+rP)`, scaling both components by public `k` yields `(krG, kmG+krP)`, which decrypts to `k·m`. An interest rate is a public loan parameter. What is genuinely non-linear is rounding and fixed-point truncation — and the remedy for that is a per-payment ZK proof, which is the pattern XLS-96 already uses. **Found independently by both REVIEW-003 legs**, and verified algebraically before acceptance. It was graded C and its inference was stated, which is what made it checkable — but a stated inference that is wrong is still wrong |
513:| R-5 | TASK ZERO §9: the vault converts the whole facility float in one transaction | ~~Retracted 2026-08-16 per ADDENDUM §1~~ — **un-retracted 2026-08-16 (same day), see F-7** | The retraction was correct about the mechanism and wrong about the consequence. A 10,000,000-unit balance is **not** undecryptable: decryption takes a caller-supplied window, and the vault — which tracks its own float off-ledger anyway — decrypts it in ~5 ms with a narrow window. §9's single-conversion design **stands**. What must be added is not a float ceiling but a **balance-tracking requirement** and a resynchronisation cost budget |
514:| R-7 | `docs/explainer/WHAT-CONFIDENTIAL-MEANS-2026-09-02.md`, limit 1, and the README before 2026-10-19: a holder who converts in 500, receives nothing and converts back 400 shows that *"100 left by transfer"* | The other 100 is either still held or has left by confidential transfer. The arithmetic bounds the balance and does not say which. Corrected in the explainer on 2026-10-21 | The README's rewrite for publication (`c561541`, 2026-10-19) carried the corrected sentence with no row, and the explainer the README links for the long form kept the old one, public from that day. Found on 2026-10-21 while drafting the hosted site's explainer from both. The error overstated what an observer learns, not what is hidden |
515:| R-8 | `web/api/capability.ts`'s wallet row and the wallet page: *"A confidential transaction needs the ElGamal secret and the account key"*, and *"needs two secrets"* | A Send, a ConvertBack and the first Convert, which registers the key, need both; a MergeInbox or a later Convert needs only the account key (TASK ZERO §3), and the facility signs its MergeInbox with the seed alone. The wallet conclusion stands, because without the first Convert there is no position. Corrected 2026-10-22 | Found by `REVIEW-061` leg A in a diagram caption that repeated the sentence. Both copies had been on the public site since publication; earlier reads of the wallet page checked its conclusion, which holds, and not this premise |

CLAIMS.md:33:| P-12 | **Any claim that a source does NOT say something — *nothing documents it*, *no such field*, *not exported*, *nobody has called it out*, *I could not find it* — without the search that would falsify it recorded alongside** | It is an assertion about a whole corpus and one search settles it. **Three graded rows failed this way on 2026-08-26** (E-155, E-156), two of them **positives**: U-15 quoted a sentence XLS-0096 has never contained; U-12 said a property was *"not called out anywhere I found"* when the spec states it **twice**, once in wording identical to the benefit claimed as an original reading. ⚠ **This shape survives review because it does not look like a claim** — a quotation has quotation marks, a benchmark visibly wants a methodology, and *"nobody has documented this"* reads as modesty. Gated by `tools/check-negative-claims.ts` |
CLAIMS.md:59:| E-18 | An `MPToken` cannot be deleted once confidential fields are initialised, even at zero balance. ⚠ **Superseded upstream on 2026-09-03.** XLS-0096 PR #604 replaced this with two conditional rules in §7.2: a holder's own deletion is blocked while the issuance's `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` is non-zero, and implicit deletion while any confidential field is present. Read at `58ccd30` on 2026-10-18 (E-316, F-29) | Spec §7.4, at the time; §7.2 at `58ccd30` for the supersession | A |
CLAIMS.md:108:| E-58 | **The auditor view is executed, not inferred.** With an issuance carrying `sfAuditorEncryptionKey`, the auditor's ElGamal key decrypts a holder's `sfAuditorEncryptedBalance` to the exact plaintext balance, matching what the holder reads with their own key and what the issuer reads from the issuer mirror. Read 500 immediately after the Convert, then **re-derived independently as 475 after a 25-unit Send** — the three views track each other through a balance change, which is the stronger statement | Executed 2026-08-17 on issuance `00453A7DD67EB7E60002C33AAD59E7F544C136A8D30F5D52`. **Three parties decrypt the same balance: holder, issuer, auditor — verified twice, at two different balances.** Reinforces P-9 and P-4 — this is a confidentiality model with mandatory issuer visibility and optional third-party visibility, and neither is hideable from the holder's side | A |
CLAIMS.md:109:| E-59 | **The two harnesses CLAUDE.md requires now exist and both self-verify.** `tools/observer-harness.ts` holds **no keys and links nothing from mpt-crypto** — no vendored C library, no WASM module. It recomputes the canonical `EncZero` from three public inputs using general-purpose primitives only, `@noble/hashes` and `@noble/curves`. *(Corrected 2026-08-17: an earlier wording said "links no cryptography library", which was false — it links two.)*, and `--assert-encrypted-zero` **exits non-zero unless F-1 is demonstrated on live ledger state**. `tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts` is the single command CLAUDE.md specifies, taking its key from the environment or a file and never from argv | Executed 2026-08-17. **The reimplementation is cross-checked, not asserted**: it reproduces an on-chain `sfConfidentialBalanceSpending` byte-for-byte, and a perturbed-AccountID control matches nothing on the same object — so the match is evidence rather than coincidence. Negative controls pass: wrong key → exit 1, absent key → exit 2, unknown flag → exit 2, missing key file → exit 2 with no path in the message. ⚠ **The rejection-sampling retry branch is NOT covered by any test** — it fires with probability ~2⁻¹²⁸, so the live cross-check does not exercise it, and this row should not be read as validating it. The issuer tool read the same balance, 475, under **three different keys** (issuer, auditor, holder) from three different mirrors. **This is the first artifact in the project that would fail if a confidentiality claim were false**, which is what CLAUDE.md's testing rule asks for. **Extended 2026-08-18 with a second assertion, `--assert-no-r-reuse`** (E-63): it scans an account's confidential transactions for a `C₁` appearing in more than one transaction — reuse of the ElGamal randomness `r` — and, where found, recovers the difference between the two hidden amounts with no key. **Discriminates in both directions, executed**: exit 1 on the account whose transactions deliberately reused `r`, with the difference recovered from all three participant ciphertexts; exit 0 on two accounts that did not. It correctly does **not** flag `C₁` repeating *within* one Send, which is by design (E-4/F-13). This is a self-check as much as an audit — our own signer could regress into it and the ledger would not complain | A |
CLAIMS.md:168:| E-117 | ⚠ **Rejecting the XLS-65 `Vault` traded away a custody property, and ~~ADR-0002 does not say so~~ ADR-0002 has said so since `c34dcec` (E-256, E-295).** A `Vault`'s pseudo-account holds pooled assets **with no key** — nothing can move them outside the protocol's rules. The Dedicated Account that replaces it is an **ordinary XRPL account with an ordinary key**, so whoever holds that key can move the entire float. Rev 4 rejected the hybrid on blocker 1 (share tokens cannot be confidential) and that reasoning stands. ⚠ ~~*the comparison table counts only disclosure and accounting; it has no row for custody*~~ **— STRUCK 2026-09-09, and it was false from the commit that wrote this row.** `c34dcec` added **both** E-117 and the `Custody of the pooled float` row it says is missing, so the clause described a state its own commit had already ended and then read as live for twenty days. The real axis is sharper than the ADR states it: **confidential positions, or non-custodial pooling — one or the other, today.** This project picks confidentiality because that is its premise, and a design needing *"no single party can move the float"* would weigh it the other way | **Noticed 2026-08-20** while answering where a `Vault` *would* fit, which is a question the ADR never asks. ⚠ **Not a reversal of rev 4** — it is a cost rev 4 does not price. ⚠ **Both premises verified 2026-09-09, and the first is STRONGER than this row states it.** rippled 3.3.0 `src/libxrpl/ledger/helpers/AccountRootHelpers.cpp:565-604`, `createPseudoAccount`: `sfFlags` is set to `lsfDisableMaster \| lsfDefaultRipple \| lsfDepositAuth` at `:597`, and `sfSequence` to **0** under `featureSingleAssetVault` at `:588-593`, over the comment *"Pseudo-accounts can't submit transactions … an extra level of protection"*. So it is not merely that nobody holds a key: the master is disabled, deposit authorisation is on, and the sequence number cannot support a transaction. The second premise is `facility/src/facility.ts:129`, which runs `checkSeedControlsAddress` over `bank` and `dedicated` alike. ~~**What is live is the last clause and only it: the facility's float sits under one key and no review has asked who holds it**~~ **Discharged 2026-09-13 (E-294): `REVIEW-046` asked who holds it and found more holders than one key, and the owner ruled B-27, accepting the risk by name with a printed disclosure.** What was live was the last clause and only it — P-12 search, 2026-09-09, over ~~357~~ 204 files in `docs/reviews/` (E-292): the only `custod` matches inside `REVIEW-019`, `REVIEW-022` and `REVIEW-023` are boilerplate quotations of P-5 in ~~two REQUEST documents~~ three REQUEST documents and one leg (E-292), and no leg or adjudication in any of the three rounds weighs it. ⚠ **A decision request now exists** ~~and this clause is still live~~ (discharged 2026-09-13, below): `docs/decisions/DECISION-REQUEST-e117-custody-of-the-float-2026-09-11.md` (2026-09-11) states the question and prices the options, and it establishes that the seed confers a **second** power this row never names — permanent, unrotatable decryption of every per-supplier amount (E-266). A request is not a review, so nothing here is discharged. CLAIMS.md:197:| E-146 | **Architecture §6 step 7 is BUILT AND EXECUTED — `facility/`, the receivables facility as application logic.** ⚠ **Executed under E-151's amendment, so `underB15Conditions: false`** — the ledger source was remote and this run says nothing about the loopback obligation. **Reviewed in two rounds, three legs → E-150.** ~~and it is NOT EXECUTED~~ — **struck 2026-08-25.** The Dedicated Account, the single conversion, supplier enrolment, drawdowns, repayments, the balance book and retirement in tranches. CLAIMS.md:326:| E-266 | **The Dedicated Account's seed confers two powers and E-117 names only one.** Power 1 is movement: drawdown or retire the whole float. Power 2 is decryption of the whole book — `ConfidentialMPTSend` carries `SenderEncryptedAmount` and `DestinationEncryptedAmount` as separate signed fields, and the Dedicated Account is an endpoint of every drawdown and every repayment by construction, so its key reads every per-supplier amount and not merely the float total. ⚠ Power 2 cannot be rotated FOR THAT ACCOUNT at any level: the key derives from the master seed, XLS-96 has neither rotation nor recovery (P-10) and a registered key is fixed for the life of the `MPToken` (E-9), so a leaked seed reads that account's whole book for ever — though a REPLACEMENT account bounds a leak to what came before it, which is the only rotation this protocol offers. ⚠ The three XRPL controls that look like the fix do not work as stated — a signer list or a regular key leaves the master-key path open and so removes nothing, `lsfDisableMaster` is the only flag that closes it and the signer refuses to build any transaction for an account carrying it, and all three flip the keygen ruling's condition 2, which **recommends** a standalone key and demands a second typed acknowledgement rather than refusing the derived one Neither power is exclusive to this seed, and the two are not independent: the key that decrypts is also required to spend (E-290). | Verified 2026-09-11, both premises at primary source. The amount fields at `signer/src/xrpl.rs:1182`, `:1184`, `:1186` — and `:1191`, the auditor arm, **only when the issuance carries an auditor key, which this facility's does not** (`facility/run-facility.ts:349-354`, appended conditionally at `signer/src/txn.rs:795-799`) — read as one ciphertext set at `signer/src/txn.rs:785-797`, sharing one `C₁` because they encrypt one value under shared randomness (`docs/task-zero/GAP-3-compact-sigma-2026-08-16.md:68`); the signer's own decrypt at `signer/src/txn.rs:39`. Both endpoints routed through `cfg.dedicated` at `facility/src/facility.ts:441`, `:446`; retire at `:516-537`. The master-path fact is read at rippled 3.3.0 `src/libxrpl/tx/Transactor.cpp` rather than inherited: `checkSign` dispatches to `checkMultiSign` only on `sfSigners` in the transaction (`:976-979`), so a singly-signed transaction from an account holding a signer list still reaches `checkSingleSign`, where the enabled master key returns `tesSUCCESS` (`:1033-1036`) and a regular key is one more accepted signer (`:1026-1030`). ⚠ That path is `src/libxrpl/tx/`, not the `src/xrpld/app/tx/detail/` an older citation would use, which 404s at this tag. CLAIMS.md:479:| F-26 | ⚠ **The facility walkthrough's *"After this, no plaintext amount appears again until the facility is retired or resized"* was FALSE, and it is the SECOND false disclosure sentence in that document.** Step 5 — supplier enrolment — publishes **two** plaintext amounts per supplier: the bank's `Payment` of the enrolment token, and the supplier's own `ConfidentialMPTConvert`, whose `MPTAmount` is a plaintext field exactly as step 4's and step 9's are. On the document's own worked example — thirty suppliers — that is **sixty plaintext amounts** between the float conversion and the first drawdown, all of them after the sentence says none appear. **What is true is narrower and is what the design actually buys**: no *drawdown* and no *repayment* publishes an amount, and those are the numbers that carry the commercial signal. ⚠ **Like F-24, it survived the document's own `claims-check`, and like F-24 it is about WHICH amounts are plaintext.** A sentence of the form *"no plaintext amount appears after X"* should be assumed wrong until every transaction type between X and the end has been listed | **Found 2026-08-25 by `REVIEW-022`'s Codex leg**, reading the walkthrough against `run-facility.ts`'s enrolment path — which repeated the same sentence, so the code and the explainer were wrong together. CLAIMS.md:480:| F-27 | **`ConfidentialMPTConvert` deposits into the holder's INBOX, not into their spendable balance — a `ConfidentialMPTMergeInbox` is required before any of it can be sent.** Read off the ledger on 2026-08-25 after a validated Convert of 5000 on `rQfHjoAYnpSSuvqoYK1eYTiBjoT3az65pB`: `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` decrypted to **0** and `ConfidentialBalanceInbox` to **5000**. ⚠ **This project had the fact in front of it three times and never wrote it down** — every earlier driver (`spike-2026-08-17/convert.ts`, `spike-step3`, `spike-step6`) performs a MergeInbox immediately after its Convert, and one of them even labels it *"not a signer operation"* — so the sequence was copied without the reason ever being recorded. CLAIMS.md:492:| F-29 | **XLS-0096 §7.4 states the `MPToken` deletion blocker unconditionally; rippled enforces it through two paths that disagree.** §7.4: a holder's `MPToken` *"cannot be deleted from the ledger once confidential fields have been initialized, even if"* all three balances hold the canonical encrypted zero. `MPTokenHelpers.cpp:300–307` (`removeEmptyHolding`) matches it — unconditional, on the presence of any of the **four** confidential fields. `MPTokenAuthorize.cpp:86–103` (`tfMPTUnauthorize`, the holder deleting their own holding) does **not**: it returns `tecHAS_OBLIGATIONS` only when the **issuance-level** `sfConfidentialOutstandingAmount != 0`, and then tests only **two** of the four fields. With issuance-wide confidential outstanding at 0, the holding is deletable with confidential fields present. ⚠ **Not a supply-accounting defect** — a zero issuance-wide total means every holder's confidential balance is provably zero, and the inline comment says that is the reasoning. **The consequence is for integrators: the reserve is NOT permanently locked, which is what §7.4's wording would have an onboarding flow tell a user.** ⚠ **P-10 is NOT weakened and must not be read as weakened**: XLS-96 still has no key rotation and no key recovery. Deleting and recreating a holding is not rotation, and the upstream filing says so explicitly before raising the question of whether the sequence is intended ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-26, same day, by a second review pass — and the correction was posted to the issue.** The conditional behaviour is **deliberate and pinned by upstream tests**: `ConfidentialTransfer_test.cpp:3156` expects `tecHAS_OBLIGATIONS` *"even if he has encrypted zero amount"* while confidential outstanding is non-zero, and `:3164` / `:3249` expect the unauthorize to **succeed** — *"can delete mptoken if outstanding confidential balance is zero"*. The unconditional path is tested too, for vault shares (`Vault_test.cpp:5972`, `ConfidentialTransfer_test.cpp:3256`). **So the two paths do not disagree by oversight; they encode different policies and both are intended, which means the defect is §7.4's WORDING and nothing else.** The filing had ended by asking a maintainer which behaviour was intended — a question the test suite answers, and which should have been checked before filing rather than after. | **2026-08-26, source reading at rippled 3.3.0. ⚠ NOT EXECUTED** — the deletion sequence has not been run on Devnet, and the filing says so in its own disclosure. CLAIMS.md:45:| E-4 | Total Send payload is **1210 B without an auditor and 1276 B with one — both now MEASURED.** The conflicting 1177 B is **wrong for the wire** | **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 by measurement.** Three `ConfidentialMPTSend` transactions serialised and accepted on standard Devnet. Each encrypted-amount field carries a **full 66-byte ElGamal pair**, not a bare 33-byte `C₂`: `SenderEncryptedAmount` 66, `DestinationEncryptedAmount` 66, `IssuerEncryptedAmount` 66, `AmountCommitment` 33, `BalanceCommitment` 33, `ZKProof` 946 = **1210 B**. The whole signed blob is **1423 B**. **Both documents were partly right and the reason matters (→ F-13):** `C₁` *is* shared, and this is **enforced by the protocol, not chosen by our harness** — a distinction REVIEW-004b HIGH-03 was right to demand and which the original evidence could not support, since it amounted to the harness observing its own input. Settled by negative control (REVIEW-004c §3): a Send built with independent per-participant randomness is refused at three layers — `mpt_get_confidential_send_proof` returns `-1` and generates nothing, `mpt_verify_send_proof` returns FAIL, and **rippled returns `tecBAD_PROOF`** where the shared-`C₁` control returns `tesSUCCESS`. The ledger nonetheless transmits `C₁` **once per participant**. The paper's 1177 is the size this would be if `C₁` were sent once, which rippled 3.3.0 does not do. The 99 B is exactly the 3 redundant 33-byte `C₁` copies at n=4. Our arithmetic was right; the paper's assumption about the wire was not. **The auditor case was measured on 2026-08-17**, closing the n=3 limitation the claims-check gate had flagged: an issuance carrying `sfAuditorEncryptionKey` was built and a four-participant Send accepted. Payload **exactly 1276 B** — `SenderEncryptedAmount` 66, `DestinationEncryptedAmount` 66, `IssuerEncryptedAmount` 66, **`AuditorEncryptedAmount` 66**, `AmountCommitment` 33, `BalanceCommitment` 33, `ZKProof` 946. Signed blob **1492 B** (n=3: 1423). The arithmetic prediction was confirmed rather than merely assumed, and **the paper's 1177 for n=4 is now refuted against a direct n=4 measurement rather than against a hypothetical** | **A on both figures, measured** |

CLAIMS.md:64:| E-22 | (a) A `ConfidentialMPTSend` of zero is **valid**. (b) It is indistinguishable from a non-zero send — **survived THREE falsification checks under a controlled rerun, NOT proven** | **Tested 2026-08-17, then RE-TESTED under control the same day after REVIEW-004 (see REVIEW-004c §2).** (a) is **Grade A, executed**: zero-amount Sends return `tesSUCCESS` and validate. (b) remains unprovable by this method; what can be said is that three attempts to break it failed. ⚠ **"Four checks" was wrong and is retired: the old check 1 was validity, which IS limb (a), so the count used the validity claim as evidence for the indistinguishability claim.** Both review legs missed this; the Gemini leg found it. Limb (b) rests on three checks, not four. **[1] Randomisation**, the sharpest test: two zero Sends from the same account at the same balance differ in **all six** observables — `SenderEncryptedAmount`, `DestinationEncryptedAmount`, `IssuerEncryptedAmount`, `AmountCommitment`, `BalanceCommitment`, `ZKProof`. Not canonical. **[2] F-1 distinguisher** — canonical `EncZero` recomputed independently for both sender and destination matches **no** zero-Send ciphertext. **[3] Side-by-side vs non-zero Sends, CONTROLLED.** The first run was confounded — both legs caught it — comparing a 100-unit send proved at balance 250 against zero sends proved at 150, with n=1 on the control and the fee dimension argued rather than measured. Re-run with zero sends **first**, since a zero send does not move the balance: **all four arms proved at balance 80**, n=2 per arm. Result: signed blob **1423 B all four**, ZKProof **946 B all four**, `AffectedNodes` **3 all four**, identical fields mutated on identical node types (`ConfidentialBalanceInbox`+`IssuerEncryptedBalance` on the destination; `ConfidentialBalanceSpending`+`ConfidentialBalanceVersion`+`IssuerEncryptedBalance` on the sender), version **+1 all four**. **Fee threshold measured on both arms**, not deduced: a full ladder gives `telINSUF_FEE_P` at 1 and 9 drops and `tesSUCCESS` at 10 for **both** zero and non-zero — identical. **Metadata is NOT constant and E-51 was wrong about it**, but the variation tracks *destination state*, not amount: at matched destination state the arms are byte-identical (1219/1219, then 1225/1225 once the destination has merged). **No asymmetry attributable to the amount was found.** Zero sends still mutate the sender's spending ciphertext, because subtracting `Enc(0, r)` changes `C₁` — so "nothing changed" is not observable either. **Never write "confirmed": four passing checks are not a proof of indistinguishability, and an observer with resources this session did not model may still separate them** | **(a) A, executed. (b) C — survived three checks under a controlled rerun, unfalsified, not proven** |

CLAIMS.md:118:| E-67 | **XLS-0096 nowhere states that the ElGamal encryption randomness `r` must not be reused across transactions. The specification is SILENT, and that is a specification gap.** B-12 closed. The only "reuse" statement about randomness points the other way — §5.4 says the `AmountCommitment` *"intentionally reuses the ElGamal ciphertext randomness as its blinding factor"*, which is deliberate reuse **within** one transaction and reads as permission. `BlindingFactor` is described (§7.2, §10.3) with no freshness, sampling or distribution requirement; the failure conditions (§7.3.1, §10.4.1) check only 32-byte length and consistency with the ciphertexts; **§13.8 "Attack Surface & Mitigations" does not list randomness reuse**; and §A.7 "What prevents replay or proof reuse?" answers about *proof* reuse via transcript binding, not randomness. rippled 3.3.0 stores nothing that could detect it — `ConfidentialMPTConvert.cpp:179` and `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack.cpp:80` read the field and pass it to `verifyRevealedAmount` | **Obtained and read 2026-08-18** — `XRPLF/XRPL-Standards`, `XLS-0096-confidential-mpt/README.md`, 971 lines, the text this repository had never held. ⚠ **This is proving a negative.** Searched `reuse`/`re-use`/`reused`/`fresh`/`random`/`randomness`/`blinding`/`nonce`/`entropy`/`CSPRNG`/`unique`/`distinct`/`unpredictab`/`independent scalar`/`per-transaction`/`each transaction`/`MUST`, and read §§5.4, 7, 10, 13 and Appendix A directly. CLAIMS.md:147:| E-96 | **A third review round was run against this project's own two-round rule, and it found a defect neither earlier round could have.** `CLAUDE.md` says a third round *"reviews the fixes to the fixes; that is where it stops paying."* It was run anyway, scoped to one argument: **round two's fixes changed `Convert`'s wire format on the strength of the adjudicator's own reading of XLS-0096 §7.2, and no second reader had seen them.** The finding: `ParsedTransaction::parse` applied `Convert`'s `HolderEncryptionKey`/`ZKProof` pairing rule to **`ConvertBack`** via a `_` wildcard. **Reading §10.3 to settle it showed the problem was three-fold** — ConvertBack has a *required* 816-byte `ZKProof`, **no `HolderEncryptionKey` field at all**, and a *required* `BalanceCommitment` the shared allowlist omitted. Power 2 is decryption of the whole book — `ConfidentialMPTSend` carries `SenderEncryptedAmount` and `DestinationEncryptedAmount` as separate signed fields, and the Dedicated Account is an endpoint of every drawdown and every repayment by construction, so its key reads every per-supplier amount and not merely the float total. ⚠ Power 2 cannot be rotated FOR THAT ACCOUNT at any level: the key derives from the master seed, XLS-96 has neither rotation nor recovery (P-10) and a registered key is fixed for the life of the `MPToken` (E-9), so a leaked seed reads that account's whole book for ever — though a REPLACEMENT account bounds a leak to what came before it, which is the only rotation this protocol offers. ⚠ The three XRPL controls that look like the fix do not work as stated — a signer list or a regular key leaves the master-key path open and so removes nothing, `lsfDisableMaster` is the only flag that closes it and the signer refuses to build any transaction for an account carrying it, and all three flip the keygen ruling's condition 2, which **recommends** a standalone key and demands a second typed acknowledgement rather than refusing the derived one Neither power is exclusive to this seed, and the two are not independent: the key that decrypts is also required to spend (E-290). | Verified 2026-09-11, both premises at primary source. 168:| E-117 | ⚠ **Rejecting the XLS-65 `Vault` traded away a custody property, and ~~ADR-0002 does not say so~~ ADR-0002 has said so since `c34dcec` (E-256, E-295).** A `Vault`'s pseudo-account holds pooled assets **with no key** — nothing can move them outside the protocol's rules. The Dedicated Account that replaces it is an **ordinary XRPL account with an ordinary key**, so whoever holds that key can move the entire float. Rev 4 rejected the hybrid on blocker 1 (share tokens cannot be confidential) and that reasoning stands. ⚠ ~~*the comparison table counts only disclosure and accounting; it has no row for custody*~~ **— STRUCK 2026-09-09, and it was false from the commit that wrote this row.** `c34dcec` added **both** E-117 and the `Custody of the pooled float` row it says is missing, so the clause described a state its own commit had already ended and then read as live for twenty days. The real axis is sharper than the ADR states it: **confidential positions, or non-custodial pooling — one or the other, today.** This project picks confidentiality because that is its premise, and a design needing *"no single party can move the float"* would weigh it the other way | **Noticed 2026-08-20** while answering where a `Vault` *would* fit, which is a question the ADR never asks. ⚠ **Not a reversal of rev 4** — it is a cost rev 4 does not price. ⚠ **Both premises verified 2026-09-09, and the first is STRONGER than this row states it.** rippled 3.3.0 `src/libxrpl/ledger/helpers/AccountRootHelpers.cpp:565-604`, `createPseudoAccount`: `sfFlags` is set to `lsfDisableMaster \| lsfDefaultRipple \| lsfDepositAuth` at `:597`, and `sfSequence` to **0** under `featureSingleAssetVault` at `:588-593`, over the comment *"Pseudo-accounts can't submit transactions … an extra level of protection"*. So it is not merely that nobody holds a key: the master is disabled, deposit authorisation is on, and the sequence number cannot support a transaction. The second premise is `facility/src/facility.ts:129`, which runs `checkSeedControlsAddress` over `bank` and `dedicated` alike. ~~**What is live is the last clause and only it: the facility's float sits under one key and no review has asked who holds it**~~ **Discharged 2026-09-13 (E-294): `REVIEW-046` asked who holds it and found more holders than one key, and the owner ruled B-27, accepting the risk by name with a printed disclosure.** What was live was the last clause and only it — P-12 search, 2026-09-09, over ~~357~~ 204 files in `docs/reviews/` (E-292): the only `custod` matches inside `REVIEW-019`, `REVIEW-022` and `REVIEW-023` are boilerplate quotations of P-5 in ~~two REQUEST documents~~ three REQUEST documents and one leg (E-292), and no leg or adjudication in any of the three rounds weighs it. ⚠ **A decision request now exists** ~~and this clause is still live~~ (discharged 2026-09-13, below): `docs/decisions/DECISION-REQUEST-e117-custody-of-the-float-2026-09-11.md` (2026-09-11) states the question and prices the options, and it establishes that the seed confers a **second** power this row never names — permanent, unrotatable decryption of every per-supplier amount (E-266). 326:| E-266 | **The Dedicated Account's seed confers two powers and E-117 names only one.** Power 1 is movement: drawdown or retire the whole float. Power 2 is decryption of the whole book — `ConfidentialMPTSend` carries `SenderEncryptedAmount` and `DestinationEncryptedAmount` as separate signed fields, and the Dedicated Account is an endpoint of every drawdown and every repayment by construction, so its key reads every per-supplier amount and not merely the float total. ⚠ Power 2 cannot be rotated FOR THAT ACCOUNT at any level: the key derives from the master seed, XLS-96 has neither rotation nor recovery (P-10) and a registered key is fixed for the life of the `MPToken` (E-9), so a leaked seed reads that account's whole book for ever — though a REPLACEMENT account bounds a leak to what came before it, which is the only rotation this protocol offers. ⚠ The three XRPL controls that look like the fix do not work as stated — a signer list or a regular key leaves the master-key path open and so removes nothing, `lsfDisableMaster` is the only flag that closes it and the signer refuses to build any transaction for an account carrying it, and all three flip the keygen ruling's condition 2, which **recommends** a standalone key and demands a second typed acknowledgement rather than refusing the derived one Neither power is exclusive to this seed, and the two are not independent: the key that decrypts is also required to spend (E-290). | Verified 2026-09-11, both premises at primary source. The amount fields at `signer/src/xrpl.rs:1182`, `:1184`, `:1186` — and `:1191`, the auditor arm, **only when the issuance carries an auditor key, which this facility's does not** (`facility/run-facility.ts:349-354`, appended conditionally at `signer/src/txn.rs:795-799`) — read as one ciphertext set at `signer/src/txn.rs:785-797`, sharing one `C₁` because they encrypt one value under shared randomness (`docs/task-zero/GAP-3-compact-sigma-2026-08-16.md:68`); the signer's own decrypt at `signer/src/txn.rs:39`. Both endpoints routed through `cfg.dedicated` at `facility/src/facility.ts:441`, `:446`; retire at `:516-537`. The master-path fact is read at rippled 3.3.0 `src/libxrpl/tx/Transactor.cpp` rather than inherited: `checkSign` dispatches to `checkMultiSign` only on `sfSigners` in the transaction (`:976-979`), so a singly-signed transaction from an account holding a signer list still reaches `checkSingleSign`, where the enabled master key returns `tesSUCCESS` (`:1033-1036`) and a regular key is one more accepted signer (`:1026-1030`). ⚠ That path is `src/libxrpl/tx/`, not the `src/xrpld/app/tx/detail/` an older citation would use, which 404s at this tag. 350:| E-290 | **The custody decision request priced the wrong set of credentials, and its first review round of its own withdrew its recommendation.** Three legs with differed packets found both halves independently, the specification-only leg included. First, the key that decrypts is also required to spend: every Send and ConvertBack proof takes the holder's ElGamal secret, so a confidential movement needs a signature and that secret together, and Option 2's *"movement moves to the quorum"* was false. Second, the Dedicated Account's key is not the only power over the float. The issuer can erase it with a confidential clawback and freeze it by enabling `CanLock` and locking, and the issuer's secret and the facility's plaintext book both read every ~~amount~~ confidential amount (E-295). Round two found a second freeze mechanism, requiring authorisation, also E-295. The option set also missed a regular key with the master disabled, which splits movement without a quorum, and delegation, a fourth authorisation path that nothing in this repository reads. ⚠ The recommendation, Option 1 with Option 2 costed and not built, is withdrawn and not replaced, because a replacement written by the reviewing session would be one more unreviewed input. | `docs/reviews/REVIEW-046c-adjudication-custody-request-2026-09-13.md` H1 to H4, each re-verified by the adjudicator after the legs returned. 427:| B-27 | **RULED 2026-09-13 by the project owner (E-294).** The question was: **who holds the Dedicated Account's key, and what the architecture says about it.** This is E-117's last live clause, and E-266 is why it is sharper than E-117 states: one ordinary seed confers both movement of the whole float and permanent decryption of every per-supplier amount, and the second of those cannot be rotated or split under XLS-96.      7	
     8	Is confidential transfer possible on the XRP Ledger? Yes, on Devnet only: read on 2026-10-22, the ConfidentialTransfer amendment (XLS-96) was enabled on Devnet, and not on Mainnet or Testnet.
     9	
    10	It hides less than the word suggests. I built the core of a receivables facility on it and ran it on Devnet to find out exactly what an outside observer sees.
    11	
    12	Hidden from the public: each holder's balance, and the amount of each transfer between confidential balances.
    13	
    14	Public: who pays whom, when, and how often. Every amount converted into or out of confidential form. Some zero balances, which anyone can recognise.
    15	
    16	The issuer can read every balance, always. No setting turns that off.
    17	
    18	There is no key rotation or recovery in XLS-96, or on Mainnet, Testnet or Devnet today. A draft standard, XLS-99, proposes both.
    19	
    20	In the facility, that means the size of the float, which suppliers drew on which days, and every retirement tranche are public. Each supplier's limit, the size of each drawdown and what each supplier owes are not. It hides the shape of the book, not its size.
    21	
    22	Along the way, eleven issues went upstream to XRPLF repositories. Four were resolved in September, and a fifth was fixed on 2026-10-21, not yet in a release.
    23	
    24	This is a research project on a test network. It is not audited, and not production-ready.
    25

## Factual checks and counts

- Blog’s dated amendment sentence is supported by the survey and E-353: `ConfidentialTransfer` was enabled on Devnet and supported but not enabled on Mainnet and Testnet. The survey records the amendment-check script at 2026-10-22T03:25:30.481Z and the later network `feature`/`server_info` reads at 03:30:47.442Z. Thus the draft’s rounded “03:25 UTC” matches the script timestamp; the post should make clear which reading it means. LinkedIn’s date-only wording is supported, though less precise.
- Disclosure claims about hidden balances and Send amounts, public identities/timing/frequency and conversion amounts, mandatory issuer visibility, and the auditor’s optional additional view agree with `CLAIMS.md` P-4/P-9/P-11, E-11 and E-58. E-58 specifically supports the quoted 500 → 475 after a 25-unit Send, decrypted by holder, issuer and auditor. E-33 supports the Send-proof key claim; E-12 supports clawback burning rather than crediting another account. E-290 supports the later-locking caveat, which the draft correctly labels as read from source, not executed.
- The fixed-zero statements cite F-1/E-59 and E-211; the public-number example is properly narrowed by R-7. The facility run’s 28 validated transactions on 2026-08-25 is supported by E-146 and its cited B-18. Its public disclosure of each ConvertBack tranche is supported by F-24; the custody limitation is supported by E-151/E-266. The `tec`/`fail_hard` example and the wrong-destination-key risk distinguish executed evidence (E-149) from source reading (F-23), as stated.
- The 61 numbered review-record count is consistent with the numbered review series through REVIEW-061; it is not a count of all files in `docs/reviews`. The eleven filings are the eleven rows in the survey Results table, across xrpl-dev-portal, XRPL-Standards, rippled and mpt-crypto. Survey outcomes support four September resolutions, #148’s October 21 closure/fix on `main` and absence from a release, #141’s reply and the approved-but-unmerged #145, open #153–155, unanswered #611, and the same-day closure of rippled #8118. The 57-day interval for #611 is as recorded in the survey; August 26 to October 22 is 57 elapsed days. The R-1–R-8 and P-1–P-12 counts are correct, but the draft’s characterization of every R entry is not (A-1). The “since mid-August” description is consistent with the project’s August-start evidence; it is approximate wording, not a count.
- The “Final since 2026-09-03” standard-author credit and Dore/Cenk protocol credit agree with E-1 and P-7. The XLS-99 draft and lack of rotation/recovery in XLS-96 and on the three named networks match E-353. The upstream outcome summaries in the blog track the survey; its three-review paragraph names Trail of Bits, the Sherlock XRP Ledger Attackathon and Zellic’s scan, and correctly says only the Trail of Bits report could be checked. The LinkedIn version omits that required context and most filing outcomes (A-2).
- Other uncited factual statements checked against the repository include the Devnet-only signer disclaimer, the transaction sequence and its published conversion amounts, the CI vocabulary controls, the project’s Apache-2.0 license, and the demo/read-only descriptions; I found no separate reportable factual error in those statements. LinkedIn’s statements on disclosures, issuer decryption, rotation/recovery, and facility exposure are supported by the corresponding P/E/F rows cited above, but it does not cite them inline.

- [P1] Qualify what the eight correction records represent — $USER_TMP/T/r062a.p1gA5h/repo/docs/posts/BLOG-2026-10-22-draft.md:237-238
  **HIGH —** The draft says, “The register keeps eight corrections, R-1 to R-8, each a sentence this project published and later found false.” That overstates the register: `CLAIMS.md` R-4 says the cited documentation behavior was real and the finding was “Not a doc error”; R-5 says the retraction was reversed and the original design “stands.” The count of eight records is right, but not all eight are false sentences later corrected. Suggested correction: call them eight correction/retraction records and distinguish the retracted, withdrawn, and reversed entries. Evidence: `CLAIMS.md:511,513`.

- [P2] Include the required audit context and filing outcomes in LinkedIn — $USER_TMP/T/r062a.p1gA5h/repo/docs/posts/LINKEDIN-2026-10-22-draft.md:22-22
  **MEDIUM —** The LinkedIn draft says, “Along the way, eleven issues went upstream to XRPLF repositories. Four were resolved in September, and a fifth was fixed on 2026-10-21, not yet in a release.” This leaves the other six filings’ survey-date outcomes unstated and omits the three reviews required by Phase 3: Trail of Bits, the Sherlock XRP Ledger Attackathon, and Zellic’s scan of mpt-crypto, including that only Trail of Bits’ report could be checked. The survey lists the outcomes (`docs/evidence/upstream-survey-2026-10-22/README.md`, Results and “Everything else”); the plan requires each filing to be described by its outcome and all three audits to be named (`docs/PLAN-PUBLIC-LAUNCH-2026-09-22.md:199-228`). Suggested correction: add the missing outcomes and audit context, or link to a compact summary that gives them. The claims register’s survey evidence is `CLAIMS.md:413`.

- [P2] Point the survey link to a path that exists on main — $USER_TMP/T/r062a.p1gA5h/repo/docs/posts/BLOG-2026-10-22-draft.md:340-340
  **MEDIUM —** The reference links to `docs/evidence/upstream-survey-2026-10-22/README.md`, but the GitHub Contents API for `nandanito/xrpl-confidential-positions` on `main` returns 404 for that path.
